                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::info[Make sure to check if the data exists]\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Before accessing some auth method's data, you'll need to check if that data exists for the user and then access it:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'if (user.identities.google !== null) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const userId = user.identities.google.id\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"You need to do this because if a user didn't sign up with some auth method, the data for that auth method will be `null`.\n"[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Let's look at the data for each of the available auth methods:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +

                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const usernameIdentity = user.identities.username\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Username that the user used to sign up, e.g. "fluffyllama"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  usernameIdentity.id\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- [Email](https://wasp.sh/docs/auth/email) data\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const emailIdentity = user.identities.email\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Email address the user used to sign up, e.g. "fluffyllama@app.com".\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  emailIdentity.id\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // `true` if the user has verified their email address.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  emailIdentity.isEmailVerified\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Datetime when the email verification email was sent.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  emailIdentity.emailVerificationSentAt\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Datetime when the last password reset email was sent.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  emailIdentity.passwordResetSentAt\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- [Google](https://wasp.sh/docs/auth/social-auth/google) data\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const googleIdentity = user.identities.google\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Google User ID for example "123456789012345678901"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  googleIdentity.id\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +

                      [32m'  const githubIdentity = user.identities.github\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // GitHub User ID for example "12345678"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  githubIdentity.id\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- [Keycloak](https://wasp.sh/docs/auth/social-auth/keycloak) data\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const keycloakIdentity = user.identities.keycloak\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Keycloak User ID for example "12345678-1234-1234-1234-123456789012"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  keycloakIdentity.id\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- [Discord](https://wasp.sh/docs/auth/social-auth/discord) data\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const discordIdentity = user.identities.discord\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Discord User ID for example "80351110224678912"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  discordIdentity.id\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"If you support multiple auth methods, you'll need to find which identity exists for the user and then access its data:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'if (user.identities.email !== null) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const email = user.identities.email.id\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'} else if (user.identities.google !== null) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const googleId = user.identities.google.id\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### `getFirstProviderUserId` Helper\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'The `getFirstProviderUserId` method returns the first user ID that it finds for the user. For example if the user has signed up with email, it will return the email. If the user has signed up with Google, it will return the Google ID.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'This can be useful if you support multiple authentication methods and you need *any* ID that identifies the user in your app.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```tsx title="src/MainPage.tsx"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { type AuthUser } from "wasp/auth"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const MainPage = ({ user }: { user: AuthUser }) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const userId = user.getFirstProviderUserId()\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="src/tasks.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const createTask: CreateTask<...>  = async (args, context) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const userId = context.user.getFirstProviderUserId()\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\\* Multiple identities per user will be possible in the future and then the `getFirstProviderUserId` method will return the ID of the first identity that it finds without any guarantees about which one it will be.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## Including the User with Other Entities\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Sometimes, you might want to include the user's data when fetching other entities. For example, you might want to include the user's data with the tasks they have created.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"We'll mention the `auth` and the `identities` relations which we will explain in more detail later in the [Entities Explained](#entities-explained) section.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::caution[Be careful about sensitive data]\n'[39m +
                      [32m"You'll need to include the `auth` and the `identities` relations to get the full auth data about the user. However, you should keep in mind that the `providerData` field in the `identities` can contain sensitive data like the user's hashed password (in case of email or username auth), so you will likely want to exclude it if you are returning those values to the client.\n"[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"You can include the full user's data with other entities using the `include` option in the Prisma queries:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="src/tasks.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const getAllTasks = (async (args, context) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return context.entities.Task.findMany({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    orderBy: { id: "desc" },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    select: {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      id: true,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      title: true,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      user: {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        include: {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'          auth: {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'            include: {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'              identities: {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'                // Including only the `providerName` and `providerUserId` fields\n'[39m +
                      [32m'                select: {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'                  providerName: true,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'                  providerUserId: true,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'                },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'              },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'            },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'          },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  })\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}) satisfies tasks.GetAllQuery<{}, {}>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"If you have some **piece of the auth data that you want to access frequently** (for example the `username`), it's best to store it at the top level of the `User` entity.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"For example, save the `username` or `email` as a property on the `User` and you'll be able to access it without including the `auth` and `identities` fields. We show an example in the [Defining Extra Fields on the User Entity](https://wasp.sh/docs/auth/overview#1-defining-extra-fields) section of the docs.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Getting Auth Data from the User Object\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'When you have the `user` object with the `auth` and `identities` fields, it can be a bit tedious to obtain the auth data (like username or Google ID) from it:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```tsx title="src/MainPage.tsx"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'function MainPage() {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return (\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    <div className="tasks">\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      {tasks.map((task) => (\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        <div key={task.id} className="task">\n'[39m +
                      [32m'          {task.title} by {task.user.auth?.identities[0].providerUserId}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        </div>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      ))}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    </div>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  )\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Wasp offers a few helper methods to access the user's auth data when you retrieve the `user` like this. They are `getUsername`, `getEmail` and `getFirstProviderUserId`. They can be used both on the client and the server.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### `getUsername`\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'It accepts the `user` object and if the user signed up with the [Username & password](https://wasp.sh/docs/auth/username-and-pass) auth method, it returns the username or `null` otherwise.                       [32m'  )\n'[39m +

                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'# Custom HTTP API Endpoints\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'In Wasp, the default client-server interaction mechanism is through [Operations](https://wasp.sh/docs/data-model/operations/overview). However, if you need a specific URL method/path, or a specific response, Operations may not be suitable for you. For these cases, you can use an `api`. Best of all, they should look and feel very familiar.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## How to Create an API\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'APIs are used to tie a JS function to a certain endpoint e.g. `POST /something/special`. They are distinct from Operations and have no client-side helpers (like `useQuery`).\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'To create a Wasp API, you must:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'1. Declare the API in Wasp using the `api` constructor\n'[39m +
                      [32m"2. Define the API's NodeJS implementation\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"After completing these two steps, you'll be able to call the API from the client code (via our `ky` wrapper), or from the outside world.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Specifying the API in Wasp\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'First, we need to declare the API in the Wasp file and you can easily do this with the `api` function:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="main.wasp.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { api, app } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { fooBar } from "./src/apis" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default app({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  spec: [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    api("GET", "/foo/bar", fooBar),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ],\n'[39m +
                      [32m'})\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::note\n'[39m +
                      [32m'When `main.wasp.ts` needs to point to your code, it uses imports like this:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { MainPage } from "./src/MainPage" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Notice the `with { type: "ref" }` part at the end of the import statement. This tells Wasp to treat the import as a reference to your app\'s code, without running the imported code. For more details and examples, see [reference imports](https://wasp.sh/docs/general/spec#reference-imports).\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Read more about the supported fields in the [API Reference](#api-reference).\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"### Defining the API's NodeJS Implementation\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::note\n'[39m +
                      [32m'To make sure the Wasp compiler generates the types for APIs for use in the NodeJS implementation, you should add your `api`s to your Wasp file first *and* keep the `wasp start` command running.\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'After you defined the API, it should be implemented as a NodeJS function that takes three arguments:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'1. `req`: Express Request object\n'[39m +
                      [32m'2. `res`: Express Response object\n'[39m +
                      [32m"3. `context`: An additional context object **injected into the API by Wasp**. This object contains user session information, as well as information about entities. The examples here won't use the context for simplicity purposes. You can read more about it in the [section about using entities in APIs](#using-entities-in-apis).\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="src/apis.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import type { FooBar } from "wasp/server/api";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const fooBar: FooBar = (req, res, context) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  res.set("Access-Control-Allow-Origin", "*"); // Example of modifying headers to override Wasp default CORS middleware.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  res.json({ msg: `Hello, ${context.user ? "registered user" : "stranger"}!` });\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::note\n'[39m +
                      [32m'The `FooBar` type is generated by Wasp based on the `api` spec above.\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### Providing Extra Type Information\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"We'll see how we can provide extra type information to an API function.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Let\'s say you wanted to create some `GET` route that would take an email address as a param, and provide them the answer to "Life, the Universe and Everything." 😀 What would this look like in TypeScript?\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Define the API in Wasp:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="main.wasp.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { api, app } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { fooBar } from "./src/apis" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default app({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  spec: [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    api("GET", "/foo/bar/:email", fooBar, { entities: ["Task"] }),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ],\n'[39m +
                      [32m'})\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"We can use the `FooBar` type to which we'll provide the generic **params** and **response** types, which then gives us full type safety in the implementation.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="src/apis.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { FooBar } from "wasp/server/api";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const fooBar: FooBar<\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  { email: string }, // params\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  { answer: number } // response\n'[39m +
                      [32m'> = (req, res, _context) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  console.log(req.params.email);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  res.json({ answer: 42 });\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## Using the API\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Using the API externally\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'To use the API externally, you simply call the endpoint using the method and path you used.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'For example, if your app is running at `https://example.com` then from the above you could issue a `GET` to `https://example/com/foo/callback` (in your browser, Postman, `curl`, another web service, etc.).\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Using the API from the Client\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'To use the API from your client, including with auth support, you can import the `api` instance from `wasp/client/api`. It is a [ky](https://github.com/sindresorhus/ky) instance pre-configured with the API base URL, authentication, and error handling. For example:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```tsx title="src/pages/SomePage.tsx"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import React, { useEffect } from "react";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { api } from "wasp/client/api";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'async function fetchCustomRoute() {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const data = await api.get("/foo/bar").json();\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  console.log(data);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const Foo = () => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  useEffect(() => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    fetchCustomRoute();\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  }, []);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return <>{/* ... */}</>;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### Making Sure CORS Works\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"APIs are designed to be as flexible as possible, hence they don't utilize the default middleware like Operations do. As a result, to use these APIs on the client side, you must ensure that CORS (Cross-Origin Resource Sharing) is enabled.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'You can do this by defining custom middleware for your APIs in the Wasp file.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'For example, an `apiNamespace` is a simple spec used to apply some `middlewareConfigFn` to all APIs under some specific path:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="main.wasp.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { apiNamespace, app } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { apiMiddleware } from "./src/apis" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default app({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  spec: [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    apiNamespace("/foo", { middlewareConfigFn: apiMiddleware }),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ],\n'[39m +
                      [32m'})\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'And then in the implementation file (returning the default config):\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="src/apis.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import type { MiddlewareConfigFn } from "wasp/server";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const apiMiddleware: MiddlewareConfigFn = (config) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return config;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'We are returning the default middleware which enables CORS for all APIs under the `/foo` path.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'For more information about middleware configuration, please see: [Middleware Configuration](https://wasp.sh/docs/advanced/middleware-config)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## Using Entities in APIs\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'In many cases, resources used in APIs will be [Entities](https://wasp.sh/docs/data-model/entities). To use an Entity in your API, add it to the `api` spec in Wasp:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="main.wasp.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { api, app } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { fooBar } from "./src/apis" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default app({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  spec: [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    api("GET", "/foo/bar", fooBar, { entities: ["Task"] }),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ],\n'[39m +
                      [32m'})\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Wasp will inject the specified Entity into the APIs `context` argument, giving you access to the Entity's Prisma API:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="src/apis.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import type { FooBar } from "wasp/server/api";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const fooBar: FooBar = async (req, res, context) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  res.json({ count: await context.entities.Task.count() });\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"The object `context.entities.Task` exposes `prisma.task` from [Prisma's CRUD API](https://www.prisma.io/docs/reference/tools-and-interfaces/prisma-client/crud).\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## Streaming Responses\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'You can use streaming responses to send data to the client in chunks as it becomes available. This is useful for:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"- **LLM responses** - Stream AI-generated content as it's produced\n"[39m +
                      [32m'- **Long-running processes** - Show progress updates in real-time\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- **Large datasets** - Send data incrementally to avoid timeouts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Creating a Streaming API\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'To create a streaming API, write a function that uses Express response methods like `res.write()` and `res.end()`:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="main.wasp.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { api, app } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { getStreamingText } from "./src/streaming" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default app({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  spec: [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    api("POST", "/api/streaming-example", getStreamingText),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ],\n'[39m +
                      [32m'})\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Don't forget to set up the CORS middleware. See the [section explaning CORS](#making-sure-cors-works) for details.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="src/streaming.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import OpenAI from "openai";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import type { GetStreamingText } from "wasp/server/api";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const client = new OpenAI({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  apiKey: process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'});\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const getStreamingText: GetStreamingText<\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  never,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  string,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  { message: string }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'> = async (req, res) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const { message } = req.body;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Set appropriate headers for streaming.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  res.setHeader("Content-Type", "text/plain; charset=utf-8");\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  res.setHeader("Transfer-Encoding", "chunked");\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const stream = await client.responses.create({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    model: "gpt-5",\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    input: `Funny response to "${message}"`,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    stream: true,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  });\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  for await (const chunk of stream) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    if (chunk.type === "response.output_text.delta") {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      // Write each chunk to the response as it arrives.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      res.write(chunk.delta);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // End the response.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  res.end();\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Consuming Streaming Responses\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'You can consume streaming responses on the client using the `api` instance from `wasp/client/api`. Since ky is built on `fetch`, you get native streaming support via the `Response.body` readable stream. The `api` instance handles authentication automatically.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```tsx title="src/StreamingPage.tsx"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { useEffect, useState } from "react";\n'[39m +

                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'# Queries\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"We'll explain what Queries are and how to use them. If you're looking for a detailed API specification, skip ahead to the [API Reference](#api-reference).\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"You can use Queries to fetch data from the server. They shouldn't modify the server's state. Fetching all comments on a blog post, a list of users that liked a video, information about a single product based on its ID... All of these are perfect use cases for a Query.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::tip\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Queries are fairly similar to Actions in terms of their API. Therefore, if you're already familiar with Actions, you might find reading the entire guide repetitive.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'We instead recommend skipping ahead and only reading [the differences between Queries and Actions](https://wasp.sh/docs/data-model/operations/actions#differences-between-queries-and-actions), and consulting the [API Reference](#api-reference) as needed.\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## Working with Queries\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"You declare queries in the Wasp file and implement them using NodeJS. Wasp not only runs these queries within the server's context but also creates code that enables you to call them from any part of your codebase, whether it's on the client or server side.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"This means you don't have to build an HTTP API for your query, manage server-side request handling, or even deal with client-side response handling and caching. Instead, just concentrate on implementing the business logic inside your query, and let Wasp handle the rest!\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'To create a Query, you must:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'1. Declare the Query in Wasp using the `query` spec.\n'[39m +
                      [32m"2. Define the Query's NodeJS implementation.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"After completing these two steps, you'll be able to use the Query from any point in your code.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Specifying Queries\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'To create a Query in Wasp, we begin with a `query` spec.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Let's declare two Queries - one to fetch all tasks, and another to fetch tasks based on a filter, such as whether a task is done:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="main.wasp.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { app, query } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { getAllTasks, getFilteredTasks } from "./src/queries" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default app({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  spec: [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    query(getAllTasks),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    query(getFilteredTasks),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ],\n'[39m +
                      [32m'})\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'If you want to know about all supported options for the `query` spec, take a look at the [API Reference](#api-reference).\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::note\n'[39m +
                      [32m'When `main.wasp.ts` needs to point to your code, it uses imports like this:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { MainPage } from "./src/MainPage" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Notice the `with { type: "ref" }` part at the end of the import statement. This tells Wasp to treat the import as a reference to your app\'s code, without running the imported code. For more details and examples, see [reference imports](https://wasp.sh/docs/general/spec#reference-imports).\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::info\n'[39m +
                      [32m"You might have noticed that we told Wasp to import Query implementations that don't yet exist. Don't worry about that for now. We'll write the implementations imported from `queries.ts` in the next section.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"It's a good idea to start with the high-level concept (the Query spec in the Wasp file) and only then deal with the implementation details (the Query's implementation in JavaScript).\n"[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"After declaring a Wasp Query, Wasp derives the Query's name from the function you pass to `query`. For example, `query(getFilteredTasks)` creates a Query named `getFilteredTasks`.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Two important things then happen:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"- Wasp **generates a server-side NodeJS function** with the Query's name.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"- Wasp **generates a client-side JavaScript function** with the Query's name (e.g., `getFilteredTasks`). This function takes a single optional argument - an object containing any serializable data you wish to use inside the Query. Wasp will send this object over the network and pass it into the Query's implementation as its first positional argument (more on this when we look at the implementations). Such an abstraction works thanks to an HTTP API route handler Wasp generates on the server, which calls the Query's NodeJS implementation under the hood.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Generating these two functions ensures a similar calling interface across the entire app (both client and server).\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Implementing Queries in Node\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Now that we've declared the Query, what remains is to implement it. We've instructed Wasp to look for the Queries' implementations in the file `src/queries.ts`, so that's where we should export them from.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Here's how you might implement the previously declared Queries `getAllTasks` and `getFilteredTasks`:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="src/queries.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { type GetAllTasks, type GetFilteredTasks } from "wasp/server/operations"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'type Task = {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  id: number\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  description: string\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  isDone: boolean\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'// our "database"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const tasks: Task[] = [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  { id: 1, description: "Buy some eggs", isDone: true },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  { id: 2, description: "Make an omelette", isDone: false },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  { id: 3, description: "Eat breakfast", isDone: false },\n'[39m +
                      [32m']\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"// You don't need to use the arguments if you don't need them\n"[39m +
                      [32m'export const getAllTasks: GetAllTasks<void, Task[]> = () => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return tasks\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"// The 'args' object is something sent by the caller (most often from the client)\n"[39m +
                      [32m'export const getFilteredTasks: GetFilteredTasks<\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  Pick<Task, "isDone">,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  Task[]\n'[39m +
                      [32m'> = (args) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const { isDone } = args\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return tasks.filter((task) => task.isDone === isDone)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::info[Payload constraints]\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Wasp uses [superjson](https://github.com/flightcontrolhq/superjson) under the hood. This means you're not limited to only sending and receiving JSON payloads.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Wasp will automatically handle the serialization and deserialization [for all the data types that superjson supports](https://github.com/flightcontrolhq/superjson#decimaljs--prismadecimal:~\\:text=Superjson%20supports%20many%20extra%20types) (like `biging`, `Date`, `Map`, `Set`, etc.), and for [Prisma.Decimal](https://www.prisma.io/docs/orm/prisma-client/special-fields-and-types#working-with-decimal).\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"As long as you're annotating your Operations with the correct automatically generated types, TypeScript ensures your payloads are valid (i.e., Wasp knows how to serialize and deserialize them).\n"[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### Type support for Queries\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Wasp automatically generates the types `GetAllTasks` and `GetFilteredTasks` based on your Wasp file's specs:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- `GetAllTasks` is a generic type automatically generated by Wasp, based on the Query spec for `getAllTasks`.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- `GetFilteredTasks` is also a generic type automatically generated by Wasp, based on the Query spec for `getFilteredTasks`.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Use these types to type the Query's implementation. It's optional but very helpful since doing so properly types the Query's context.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'In this case, TypeScript will know the `context.entities` object must include the `Task` entity. TypeScript also knows whether the `context` object includes user information (it depends on whether your Query uses auth).\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'The generated types are generic and accept two optional type arguments: `Input` and `Output`.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'1. `Input` - The argument (the payload) received by the Query function.\n'[39m +
                      [32m"2. `Output` - The Query function's return type.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Use these type arguments to type the Query's inputs and outputs.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Explanation for the example above\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"The above code says that the Query `getAllTasks` doesn't expect any arguments (its input type is `void`), but it does return a list of tasks (its output type is `Task[]`).\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'On the other hand, the Query `getFilteredTasks` expects an object of type `{ isDone: boolean }`. This type is derived from the `Task` entity type.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"If you don't care about typing the Query's inputs and outputs, you can omit both type arguments. TypeScript will then infer the most general types (`never` for the input and `unknown` for the output).\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Specifying `Input` or `Output` is completely optional, but we highly recommended it. Doing so gives you:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- Type support for the arguments and the return value inside the implementation.\n'[39m +
                      [32m"- **Full-stack type safety**. We'll explore what this means when we discuss calling the Query from the client.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Read more about type support for implementing Queries in the [API Reference](#implementing-queries).\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::tip[Inferring the return type]\n'[39m +
                      [32m"If don't want to explicitly type the Query's return value, the `satisfies` keyword tells TypeScript to infer it automatically:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```typescript\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const getFoo = (async (_args, context) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const foos = await context.entities.Foo.findMany()\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    foos,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    message: "Here are some foos!",\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    queriedAt: new Date(),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}) satisfies GetFoo\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'From the snippet above, TypeScript knows:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'1. The correct type for `context`.\n'[39m +
                      [32m"2. The Query's return type is `{ foos: Foo[], message: string, queriedAt: Date }`.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"If you don't need the context, you can skip specifying the Query's type (and arguments):\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```typescript\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const getFoo = () => ({ name: "Foo", date: new Date() })\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'For a detailed explanation of the Query definition API (more precisely, its arguments and return values), check the [API Reference](#api-reference).\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Using Queries\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### Using Queries on the client\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'To call a Query on the client, you can import it from `wasp/client/operations` and call it directly.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"The usage doesn't change depending on whether the Query is authenticated or not. Wasp authenticates the logged-in user in the background.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { getAllTasks, getFilteredTasks } from "wasp/client/operations"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +

                      [32m'// the payloads.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const allTasks = await getAllTasks()\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const doneTasks = await getFilteredTasks({ isDone: true })\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Wasp supports **automatic full-stack type safety**. You only need to specify the Query's type in its server-side definition, and the client code will automatically know its API payload types.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +

                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::note\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Last checked with Wasp 0.24 and multer 2.1.1.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'This guide depends on external libraries or services, so it may become outdated over time. We do our best to keep it up to date, but make sure to check their documentation for any changes.\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'This guide shows you how to implement file uploads in your Wasp application using [Multer](https://github.com/expressjs/multer).\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## Setting up File Uploads\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### 1. Install Multer\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Install the Multer package and its types:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```bash\n'[39m +
                      [32m'npm install multer\n'[39m +
                      [32m'npm install --save-dev @types/multer\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### 2. Define the API endpoint in main.wasp.ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Create an API namespace with middleware configuration and the upload endpoint:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="main.wasp.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { api, apiNamespace, app, page, route } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { configureFileUploadMiddleware, uploadFile } from "./src/apis" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { MainPage } from "./src/MainPage" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default app({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  spec: [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    route("RootRoute", "/", page(MainPage)),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    apiNamespace("/api/upload", { middlewareConfigFn: configureFileUploadMiddleware }),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    api("POST", "/api/upload", uploadFile),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ],\n'[39m +
                      [32m'})\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### 3. Create the API handlers\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Create the middleware configuration and upload handler:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="src/apis.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import type { MiddlewareConfigFn } from "wasp/server";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import type { UploadFile } from "wasp/server/api";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import multer from "multer";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const upload = multer({ dest: "uploads/" });\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const configureFileUploadMiddleware: MiddlewareConfigFn = (config) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  config.set("multer", upload.single("file"));\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return config;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const uploadFile: UploadFile = (req, res) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  console.log(req.body);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  console.log(req.file);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const file = req.file!;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return res.json({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    fileExists: !!file,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  });\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### 4. Create the upload form\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Create a form component to handle file uploads:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```tsx title="src/MainPage.tsx"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { useState } from "react";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { api } from "wasp/client/api";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const MainPage = () => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const [name, setName] = useState("");\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const [file, setFile] = useState<File>();\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const handleSubmit = async (e: React.FormEvent) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    e.preventDefault();\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    if (!file) return;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    const formData = new FormData();\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    formData.append("name", name);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    formData.append("file", file);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    const data = await api\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      .post("/api/upload", { body: formData })\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      .json<{ fileExists: boolean }>();\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    alert(JSON.stringify(data, null, 2));\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  };\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return (\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit}>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <input\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        type="text"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        placeholder="Name"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        value={name}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        onChange={(e) => setName(e.target.value)}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      />\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <input type="file" onChange={(e) => setFile(e.target.files?.[0])} />\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <button type="submit">Upload</button>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    </form>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  );\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## Customizing Upload Settings\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Change upload destination\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'You can customize where files are stored:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const upload = multer({ dest: "my-custom-uploads/" });\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Limit file size\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Add file size limits:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const upload = multer({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  dest: "uploads/",\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  limits: {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    fileSize: 5 * 1024 * 1024, // 5MB limit\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'});\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Filter file types\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Only accept certain file types:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const upload = multer({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  dest: "uploads/",\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  fileFilter: (req, file, cb) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    if (file.mimetype.startsWith("image/")) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      cb(null, true);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    } else {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      cb(new Error("Only images are allowed"));\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'});\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Handle multiple files\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'To handle multiple file uploads:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const configureFileUploadMiddleware: MiddlewareConfigFn = (config) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  config.set("multer", upload.array("files", 10)); // Max 10 files\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return config;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'For more options, see the [Multer documentation](https://github.com/expressjs/multer).\n'[39m +
                      [32m''[39m
                  }
                ]

                      [32m'## Example code\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Below you can find a starting point for making your own UI in the client code. You can customize any of its look and behaviour, just make sure to call the `signup()` or `login()` functions imported from `wasp/client/auth`.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Sign-up\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```tsx title="src/pages/auth.tsx"\n'[39m +
                      [32m"import { login, signup } from 'wasp/client/auth'\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"import { useState } from 'react'\n"[39m +
                      [32m"import { useNavigate } from 'react-router'\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export function SignupPage() {\n'[39m +
                      [32m"  const [username, setUsername] = useState('')\n"[39m +
                      [32m"  const [password, setPassword] = useState('')\n"[39m +
                      [32m'  const [error, setError] = useState<Error | null>(null)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const navigate = useNavigate()\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  async function handleSubmit(event: React.FormEvent<HTMLFormElement>) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    event.preventDefault()\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    setError(null)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    try {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      await signup({ username, password })\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      await login({ username, password })\n'[39m +
                      [32m"      navigate('/')\n"[39m +
                      [32m'    } catch (error: unknown) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      setError(error as Error)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return (\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit}>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      {error && <p>Error: {error.message}</p>}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <input\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        type="text"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        autoComplete="username"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        value={username}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        onChange={(e) => setUsername(e.target.value)}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        placeholder="Username"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      />\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <input\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        type="password"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        value={password}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        onChange={(e) => setPassword(e.target.value)}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        placeholder="Password"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      />\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <button type="submit">Sign Up</button>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    </form>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  )\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### Login\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```tsx title="src/pages/auth.tsx"\n'[39m +
                      [32m"import { login } from 'wasp/client/auth'\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"import { useState } from 'react'\n"[39m +
                      [32m"import { useNavigate } from 'react-router'\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export function LoginPage() {\n'[39m +
                      [32m"  const [username, setUsername] = useState('')\n"[39m +
                      [32m"  const [password, setPassword] = useState('')\n"[39m +
                      [32m'  const [error, setError] = useState<Error | null>(null)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const navigate = useNavigate()\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  async function handleSubmit(event: React.FormEvent<HTMLFormElement>) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    event.preventDefault()\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    setError(null)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    try {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      await login({ username, password })\n'[39m +
                      [32m"      navigate('/')\n"[39m +
                      [32m'    } catch (error: unknown) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      setError(error as Error)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return (\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit}>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      {error && <p>Error: {error.message}</p>}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <input\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        type="text"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        autoComplete="username"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        value={username}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        onChange={(e) => setUsername(e.target.value)}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        placeholder="Username"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      />\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <input\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        type="password"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        value={password}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        onChange={(e) => setPassword(e.target.value)}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        placeholder="Password"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      />\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <button type="submit">Sign Up</button>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    </form>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  )\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## API Reference\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'You can import the following functions from `wasp/client/auth`:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### `login()`\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'An action for logging in the user.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'It takes one argument:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- `data: object` required\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  It has the following fields:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  - `username: string` required\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  - `password: string` required\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::note\n'[39m +
                      [32m'When using the exposed `login()` function, make sure to implement your redirect on success login logic (e.g. redirecting to home).\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### `signup()`\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'An action for signing up the user. This action does not log in the user, you still need to call `login()`.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'It takes one argument:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- `data: object` required\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  It has the following fields:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  - `username: string` required\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  - `password: string` required\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::info\n'[39m +
                      [32m'By default, Wasp will only save the `username` and `password` fields. If you want to add extra fields to your signup process, read about [defining extra signup fields](https://wasp.sh/docs/auth/overview#customizing-the-signup-process).\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m''[39m
                  }
                ]
              },
              providerOptions: {
                vertex: {

                      [32m'import { app, page, route } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +

                      [32m'import { app, page, route } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +

                      [32m'## Lazy-Loaded Routes\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"By default, Wasp lazy-loads all page routes using React Router's [`lazy`](https://reactrouter.com/how-to/code-splitting) property. This means each page's code is only downloaded when the user navigates to it, resulting in smaller initial bundle sizes. This is especially useful for apps with many routes.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'If you need a specific route to be eagerly loaded (included in the main bundle), you can set `lazy: false` on the route spec:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="main.wasp.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { app, page, route } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { DashboardPage } from "./src/DashboardPage" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"// This route's page will be included in the initial bundle\n"[39m +
                      [32m'export default app({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  spec: [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    route("DashboardRoute", "/dashboard", page(DashboardPage), { lazy: false }),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ],\n'[39m +
                      [32m'})\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::note\n'[39m +
                      [32m"Most apps won't need to change this. Disabling lazy loading is useful when you want to avoid the brief loading delay for a page that users navigate to very frequently, at the cost of a larger initial download.\n"[39m +

                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## Protecting a page with `authRequired`\n'[39m +

                      [32m'```ts title="main.wasp.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { app, page, route } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import Main from "./src/pages/Main" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default app({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  spec: [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    route("MainRoute", "/", page(Main, { authRequired: true })),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ],\n'[39m +
                      [32m'})\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::caution[Requires auth method]\n'[39m +
                      [32m'You can only use `authRequired` if your app uses one of the [available auth methods](#available-auth-methods).\n'[39m +
                      [32m':::\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"If `authRequired` is set to `true`, the page's React component (passed as the first argument to `page(...)`) receives the `user` object as a prop. Read more about the `user` object in the [Accessing the logged-in user section](#accessing-the-logged-in-user).\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## Logout action\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"We provide an action for logging out the user. Here's how you can use it:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```tsx title="src/components/LogoutButton.tsx"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { logout } from "wasp/client/auth"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const LogoutButton = () => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return <button onClick={logout}>Logout</button>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'## Accessing the logged-in user\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"You can get access to the `user` object both on the server and on the client. The `user` object contains the logged-in user's data.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'The `user` object has all the fields that you defined in your `User` entity. In addition to that, it will also contain all the auth-related fields that Wasp stores. This includes things like the `username` or the email verification status. For example, if you have a user that signed up using an email and password, the `user` object might look like this:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const user = {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // User data\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  id: "cluqsex9500017cn7i2hwsg17",\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  address: "Some address",\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Auth methods specific data\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  identities: {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    email: {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      id: "user@app.com",\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      isEmailVerified: true,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      emailVerificationSentAt: "2024-04-08T10:06:02.204Z",\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      passwordResetSentAt: null,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'You can read more about how the `User` is connected to the rest of the auth system and how you can access the user data in the [Accessing User Data](https://wasp.sh/docs/auth/entities) section of the docs.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### On the client\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'There are two ways to access the `user` object on the client:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- the `user` prop\n'[39m +
                      [32m'- the `useAuth` hook\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### Getting the `user` in authenticated routes\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"If the page's spec sets `authRequired` to `true`, the page's React component receives the `user` object as a prop. This is the simplest way to access the user inside an authenticated page:\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```ts title="main.wasp.ts"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { app, page, route } from "@wasp.sh/spec"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import Account from "./src/pages/Account" with { type: "ref" }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default app({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  spec: [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    route("AccountRoute", "/account", page(Account, {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      authRequired: true\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    })),\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ],\n'[39m +
                      [32m'})\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```tsx title="src/pages/Account.tsx"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import type { AuthUser } from "wasp/auth";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import Button from "./Button";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { logout } from "wasp/client/auth";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'const AccountPage = ({ user }: { user: AuthUser }) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return (\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    <div>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <Button onClick={logout}>Logout</Button>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      {JSON.stringify(user, null, 2)}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    </div>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  );\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default AccountPage;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### Getting the `user` in non-authenticated routes\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Wasp provides a React hook you can use in the client components - `useAuth`.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"This hook is a thin wrapper over Wasp's `useQuery` hook and returns data in the same format.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```tsx title="src/pages/MainPage.tsx"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { useAuth, logout } from "wasp/client/auth";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { Link } from "react-router";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import Todo from "../Todo";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export function Main() {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const { data: user } = useAuth();\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  if (!user) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    return (\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <span>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        Please <Link to="/login">login</Link> or{" "}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        <Link to="/signup">sign up</Link>.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      </span>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    );\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  } else {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    return (\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        <button onClick={logout}>Logout</button>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'        <Todo />\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      </>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    );\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'}\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### On the server\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### Using the `context.user` object\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"When authentication is enabled, all [queries and actions](https://wasp.sh/docs/data-model/operations/overview) have access to the `user` object through the `context` argument. `context.user` contains all User entity's fields and the auth identities connected to the user.
